how &#x27;s it going ? hello friend , my name is carl simmons . in october 1988 , i received this bulletin in the mail . naturally i was skeptical , but i decided to try it because i was heavily in debt at the time . i had everything to gain and nothing to lose . even if this program worked only a fraction of what the example showed , i would be a success ! well , i m adding this note here to tell you it does work ! i will never forget how it happened . i followed the instructions exactly . the total cost was about $ 46 . 00 including postage . i dropped the envelopes off a the post office on my way to work one day in late october hoping to earn a few dollars to buy christmas presents . the recipe i ordered from the person in the # 1 position came in about a week . i did not think much about it after that until a few weeks later when my boss told me my wife had called and i was needed at home immediately due to some emergency . i raced home in a panic , threw the apartment door open , and what a sight i saw ! my wife was standing over a pile of envelopes that the mailman had dumped on the living room floor from two big canvas bags . we spend the rest of the day and most of the night opening and counting 3 , 245 envelopes , each containing $ 2 . 00 and a self-addressed stamped envelope . the next day we received 4 , 274 more . by the time the orders stopped coming in , about two weeks later , i had received $ 92 , 428 . 00 in cash ! ( i wonder what the printer though when i had him print 46 , 000 copies of the recipes in one week ! ) needless to say , i immediately quit my job , paid off al my debts in full and started another mailing . to date my revenues are approaching $ 1 , 000 , 000 . 00 in cold hard cash . believe me , you can do the same thing . this is you lucky break that you have been hoping for all your life . you can make your dreams come true . . . do not pass it up ! here is what happens when only 10 % of the people respond to your mailing . you send out at least 200 with your name in position # 5 . 20 people send out at least 200 with your name in position # 4 . 400 people send out at least 200 with your name in position # 3 . 8000 people send out at least 200 with your name in position # 2 . 160000 people send out at least 200 with your name in position # 1 . 168420 x $ 2 . 00 each = $ 336840 in about 60 to 90 days ! the secret to making thousands for yourself . read this bulletin carefully and then reread it again . think about it , do the math and consider the wealth potential this holds for you . then send $ 2 . 00 cash and a self-addressed stamped envelope today to the addresses in positions # 1 below . go for it , order those recipes today . 1 . chocolate cream pie 2 . pineapple bavarian pudding henry w . frye , sr . harold lookwood 24684 atwood ave . 123 skokorat st . moreno valley , ca 92553 seymour , ct . 06483 3 . chocolate walnut cookies 4 . cheese garlic biscuits vincent myers , jr . dlh enterprises inc . 848 n . rainbow , # 351 p . o . box 5854 las vegas , nv 89107 san jose , ca 95150 5 . magic cookies bar acw enterprises p . o . box 3004-332 corvallis , or 97333 i tried a similar program in which the cost was $ 5 . 00 . in that one , the response was only about 3 % . the first time i sent out this recipe program a got my first responses in just one week . i sent out 250 letters instead of just 100 so my percentage of gains was higher . i am trying again with 500 letters to see if i can surpass the $ 141 , 236 . 00 of the last time . good luck to all of you . it really works ! 32 Easy Ways To Breath New Life Into Any Webpage
=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=Â Â Â Â Â  It&#x27;s true.
Ask the CEOs of Yahoo.com and America Online.Â  They&#x27;ll
tell you it&#x27;s true.Â  Send an email to Terry Dean or Allen
Says or Jim Daniels and ask them about it.Â  They&#x27;ll agree
100% that it&#x27;s true.Â  Don&#x27;t just take my word for it.
In fact, you can contact any of the 10,000 folks online
selling web marketing resources, and they will all tell you
emphatically, without question, no doubts whatsoever, that
it is absolutely true.
It&#x27;s true. ANYONE can earn a living online.Â  Really, they
can.Â  But, it takes several very important components to
join the 5% who are successful on the web.
Â Â Â Â  One of those necessities is a website.
Â Â Â Â  Now, your website does one of two things...
Â Â Â Â  ...it either makes the sale, or it doesn&#x27;t.
For 95% of online businesses, their websites simply do
not produce results.
And there is a very simple reason for poor performance.
Poor sales letters.
Does your website convince people to make a purchase?
If not, here are 32 easy ways to breathe new life into
your sales letter...
1) Write your sales letter with an individual in mind.
Go ahead and pick out someone, a real person to write your
sales letter to.Â  Doesn&#x27;t matter if it is grandma or your
next door neighbor or your cat.Â  Write your sales letter
just like you are writing it to them personally.Â  Why?
Because when your potential customer reads, it then it will
seem personal, almost like you wrote it with them in mind.
Too often, sales letters are written as if they were going
to be read to an audience rather than one person.Â  Keep your
sales letters personal, because one person at a time is
going to read them.
2) Use an illustration to get your point across.Â  In my
sales letters I have told stories about my car stalling on
the side of the road to illustrate the idea that we must
constantly add the fuel of advertising to keep our
businesses running. I have compared the hype of easily
making millions online to the chances of me riding bareback
across Montana on a grizzly bear.Â  Leads have read of how
getting to the top of an oak tree relates to aggressively
marketing online. People love a good story that pounds home
a solid message.Â  Tell stories that illustrate a point you
are trying to make.Â  Emphasize a benefit by sharing an
account from the &quot;real world.&quot;Â  It effectively creates
interest and further establishes the point.
3) Create an interest in the reader from the very first
line.Â  Your first line of the sales letter should
immediately create a desire in the reader to want to know
more.Â  Go back to the beginning of this article.Â  The first
words were, &quot;It&#x27;s true.&quot;Â  I can guarantee you that either
consciously or subconsciously you thought &quot;What&#x27;s true?&quot;
Immediately, your mind wanted to know what I was talking
about.Â  Before you even knew it you were right here, 8
paragraphs into this article.Â  Carefully craft your first
line.Â  If you can immediately get them wanting to know more,
you&#x27;ve got a winner.
4) Use bullets. People spend a lot of time reading bulleted
lists. In fact, they often reread them over and over. Use
bulleted lists to stress the benefits of your product or
service, to spell out exactly what is included in your offer.
Use an extra space in between each bullet to really highlight
each line and create a sense of more length to the list.
5) Launch into a bullet list immediately.Â  Shortly after
your opening line, immediately give the reader a bullet
list of benefits to absorb.Â  Hit them with your best shot.
Pull out the big guns and stress &quot;just a few of&quot; the most
important things the reader will discover.Â  By offering a
killer list early in your sales letter, you will automatically
create a desire in the reader to continue through your ad
copy.Â  After all, if they are already interested after the
first list of benefits, they will certainly be open to
finding out even more reasons why your product or service
will aid them.
6) Just let it all flow out.Â  Write down everything that
enters your mind as you are writing your sales letter. You
can edit it later.Â  If you just sit and start writing
everything you know about your product or service and how it
will benefit your customer, you will be amazed at how much
information floods your mind.Â  Write it ALL down.Â  Then read
through it - you&#x27;ll be able to add a lot more detail to
many of the points.Â  Edit it after you have exhausted all
of your ideas.
7) Make your sales letter personal.Â  Make sure that the
words &quot;you&quot; and &quot;your&quot; are at least 4:1 over &quot;I&quot; and &quot;my.&quot;
Your ad copy must be written about YOUR CUSTOMER not
yourself.Â  I&#x27;m not sure how the old advertising adage goes,
but it&#x27;s something like this, &quot;I don&#x27;t care a thing about
your lawn mower, I just care about my lawn.&quot;Â  Leads aren&#x27;t
interested in you or your products, they are interested in
themselves and their wants and needs.Â  When you are finished
with your sales letter and have uploaded it to a test
webpage, run a check at http://www.keywordcount.com and see
what the ratio between &quot;you&quot; and &quot;your&quot; versus references to
&quot;I,&quot; &quot;me,&quot; &quot;my,&quot; etc.Â  It&#x27;s a free service.Â  Make sure it&#x27;s
at least 4:1 in favor of the customer.
8) Write like you speak.Â  Forget all of those rules that
your grammar teacher taught you.Â  Write your sales letters
in everyday language, just like you would talk in person.
Don&#x27;t be afraid to begin sentences with &quot;And&quot; or &quot;Because.&quot;
Don&#x27;t worry about ending a sentence with a preposition.
Write like you speak. Your sales letter isn&#x27;t the great
American novel, so don&#x27;t write it like you are Ernest
Hemingway.
9) Use short paragraphs consisting of 2-4 sentences each.
Long copy works... but long paragraphs do not.Â  Use short
paragraphs that lead into the next paragraph. Don&#x27;t be
afraid to use short sentences.Â  Like this one.
Or this.
See what I mean?Â  Shorter paragraphs keep the interest of
the reader. Longer paragraphs cause eye strain and often
force the reader to get distracted.
10)Â  Stress the benefits, not the features.Â  Again,
readers want the burning question answered, &quot;What&#x27;s in it
forÂ  me?&quot;Â  What need is it going to meet? What want is it
going to fill?Â  How is your product or service going to be
of value or benefit to the reader?Â  Spell it out.Â  Don&#x27;t
focus on the features of your product or service , but
rather how those features will add value to the life of your
reader.
For example:Â  If you are selling automobile tires, you may
very well have the largest assortment of tires in the world,
but who cares? I don&#x27;t care about your selection. But, I
do care about keeping my 3-month-old baby girl safe while
we are traveling.Â  So, instead of focusing on your selection,
you focus on the fact that my baby girl can be kept safe
because you have a tire that will fit my car. You&#x27;re not
selling tires, you&#x27;re selling safety for my family. Stress
the benefits, not the features.
11) Keep the reader interested.Â  Some sales letters read
like they are a manual trying to explain to me how I can
perform some complicated surgery on my wife. They are
filled with words and phrases that I need a dictionary to
understand.Â  Unless you are writing to a very targeted
audience, avoid using technical language that many readers
might not understand. Keep it simple, using words, language
and information that are easy to understand and follow.
12) Target your sales letter.Â  When you are finished with
your final draft of the sales letter, target it to a
specific audience.Â  For example: If you are selling a &quot;work
at home&quot; product, then rewrite the sales letter by adding
words in the headlines and ad copy that are targeted towards
women who are homemakers.Â  Then, rewrite the same sales
letter and target it to college students.Â  Write another
letter targeting senior citizens.Â  Still another could be
written to high school teachers wanting to earn extra income
during summer vacation.Â  The possibilities are endless.
All you need to do is add a few words here and there in
your ad copy to make it appear that your product or service
is specifically designed for a target audience. &quot;Work only
5 hours a week,&quot; would become &quot;College Students, work only
5 hours a week.&quot;Â  Your sales letter is now targeted. Upload
all of the sales letters to separate pages on your website
(you could easily target 100&#x27;s of groups).
Then, simply advertise the targeted pages in targeted
mediums.Â  You could advertise the &quot;College Students&quot; page
in a campus ezine.Â  The &quot;Senior Citizens&quot; page could be
advertised at a retirement community message board.
By creating these targeted sales letters, you can literally
open up dozens of new groups to sell your existing product
to.Â  And, in their eyes, it looks like the product was a
match made for them.
13) Make your ad copy easy to follow.Â  Use short sentences
and paragraphs. Break up the sales letter with attention
grabbing headlines that lead into the next paragraph. One
thing that I have always found to work very well in sales
letters...
Â Â Â Â  ...is to use a pause like this.
Start the sentence on one line, leaving the reader wanting
to know more, and then finishing up on the next line. Also,
if you are going to use a sales letter that continues on
several different pages of your website, use a catchy hook
line at the end of each page to keep them clicking. &quot;Let&#x27;s
get you started down the road to success, shall we? CLICK
HERE to continue.&quot;
14) Use similes and metaphors for effect.Â  When the
customer purchases your product, they will generate &quot;a flood
of traffic that would make Noah start building another ark.&quot;
If they do not order today, then they will &quot;feel like a cat
that let the mouse get away.&quot;Â  Use words to create a picture
in the readers&#x27; mind.Â  When you think of Superman, what
comes to mind?Â  Immediately, we remember that he is &quot;faster
than a speeding bullet.&quot;Â  &quot;More powerful than a locomotive.&quot;
&quot;Able to leap tall buildings in a single bound.&quot; See how
word pictures stick in our minds?
15) Focus on one product or service.Â  Don&#x27;t try to sell
your customer multiple products at the same time. It only
confuses the reader.Â  Keep your ad copy directed at one
specific product or service.Â  Then, use other products and
services as back-end products.
16) Make it stand out.Â  Don&#x27;t kid yourself.Â  There are
hundreds, maybe thousands out there on the web doing the
same thing you are doing.Â  How will you stand out among the
crowd?Â  Your sales letter must inject personality. It must
breathe of originality.Â  Your product or service is
different. It&#x27;s not like all of the rest.Â  It is unique.
Right? Your sales letter must separate you from the
competition.Â  It must create a feeling of &quot;You won&#x27;t find
this anywhere else.&quot;
17) Be believable. &quot;Earn $54,000 in the next 24 hours!!!&quot;
Delete.Â  Good grief, do they think I am an idiot or
something?Â  Get real.Â  Don&#x27;t make outrageous claims that
are obviously not the truth.Â  You&#x27;ll ruin your reputation.
Let me tell you a simple universal fact that cannot be
reversed.Â  Once you have been branded a liar, you will
NEVER be anything but a liar.Â  It doesn&#x27;t matter if you
launch the most respectable, honest business available
anywhere, people will always have doubt because they
remember the crazy stuff you&#x27;ve said before.Â  Be believable.
Don&#x27;t exaggerate, mislead, stretch or distort the truth.
18) Be specific. Don&#x27;t generalize your information, but
rather be EXACT.Â  Instead of &quot;over 100 tips for losing
weight&quot; use &quot;124 tips for losing weight.&quot; By generalizing
information, it creates doubt and questions in the reader&#x27;s
mind.Â  &quot;What am I really getting here?Â  Does he even know?&quot;
When you use specific information, the reader begins to
think, &quot;This person must have counted.Â  I know exactly what
I can expect.&quot;Â  &quot;Platitudes and generalities roll off the
human understanding like water from a duck,&quot; wrote Claude
Hopkins in his classic book &quot;Scientific Advertising.&quot; &quot;They
leave no impression whatsoever.&quot;
19) Be complete. Tell the reader everything they would
want to know about your product or service.Â  Answer all of
their questions, anything they would want to consider before
making a purchase.Â  Think about it from their point of view.
Ask yourself, &quot;Why wouldn&#x27;t I buy this?&quot;Â  Then, address that
in your sales letter.Â  Remove anything that would keep the
reader from making the purchase.
20) Use testimonials to boost your sales.Â  Share actual
excerpts from what your current customers are saying about
your product or service. Many websites have an entire
section or even a separate page that has endorsements and
compliments listed.Â  Satisfied customers remove some of the
doubt in the mind of the reader.Â  &quot;If these people have
found a lot of value and benefit in the product, then I
probably will too.&quot;Â  Especially effective are testimonials
from respected, well known &quot;authorities&quot; within your target
field.
21) Use headlines over and over throughout the sales letter.
A headline isn&#x27;t just relegated to the beginning of your
ad copy.Â  Use them frequently -but don&#x27;t overuse.Â  A well-
placed headline re-grabs the reader&#x27;s attention, brings
them deeper into the letter, and readies them for the next
paragraph. You will want to spend as much time working on
your headlines as you do the entire sales letter.Â  They are
that important.
22) Avoid asking stupid questions.Â  &quot;Wouldn&#x27;t you like to
make $1,000,000 a year?&quot; &quot;Doesn&#x27;t that sound great?&quot; &quot;Would
you like to be as successful as I am?&quot;Â  Avoid any question
that insults the intelligence of your reader or makes them
feel like they are inferior.
23) Offer a freebie even if the customer doesn&#x27;t buy.
If the customer decides he or she isn&#x27;t going to make a
purchase, then you want to follow-up with them later to try
to influence them to buy in the future.Â  By offering a free
item, you canÂ  request their email address in order to
obtain the freebie.Â  By doing this, you can now follow-up
with the customer for a potential future sale.
Additionally, you can continue the sales process by having
your ad copy, banners, flyers, etc. within the free item.
And, of course, if your free item is a high quality, useful
product or service which impresses the customer, they
probably will be back as a customer soon.
24) Use bonuses to overwhelm the reader.Â  One of the things
that I have found very effective in writing sales letters
is to include bonus items that OUT-VALUE the actual product
I am offering.Â  Ginsu made this one famous. They were
selling a set of steak knives, but before the commercial
was finished, you had so many bonus items on the table
it was hard to refuse. Make sure you provide quality
bonuses and not some worthless, outdated junk that damages
the credibility of your main offer.
25) Use connective phrases like &quot;But wait, there&#x27;s more&quot;
and &quot;But that&#x27;s not all.&quot;Â  These phrases effectively lead
the reader from one paragraph to the next, particularly
when the next paragraph is a bullet list of benefits, or
leads into bonus items.Â  Again, the idea is MORE and MORE
value and benefits to the reader.
26) Always include a deadline.Â  By including a deadline,
you create a sense of urgency in the mind of the customer.
&quot;If I don&#x27;t order within 24 hours, then I won&#x27;t get the
bonuses.&quot;Â  &quot;Oh no, there are only 10 items remaining,
I&#x27;ve got to hurry.&quot;Â  Let the customer know what they will
be missing out on if they don&#x27;t make the deadline. Remember,
they won&#x27;t miss out on your products or bonuses, they will
miss out on all of the benefits of your products. Deadlines
are very effective.Â  Every sales letter should have one.
27) Tell them exactly how to order.Â  Be clear as to the
order process. Point them towards the order link.Â  Tell
them what methods you offer. (I.E. credit cards, checks,
etc.)Â  Make this process as simple and clear as can be.
If it takes more than 2 steps, most people won&#x27;t continue.
28)Â  Explain when the product will be delivered.Â  How
quickly will the order be processed?Â  When will the order be
available?Â  Let the customer know exactly what they can
expect when they place their order.Â  The more specific you
can be here, the better.Â  Let them know that you have a
system in place.Â  &quot;Operators are standing by.&quot;Â  Their order
will be handled properly.Â  Tell them.
29) Offer a money back guarantee.Â  Take away their last
reason to hold back.Â  Offer a &quot;no questions asked&quot; 30 day
guarantee.Â  Most people may not realize this, but in most
cases, it&#x27;s the law of the land.Â  You are REQUIRED to give
them their money back if they are not satisfied with the
product or service.Â  Since it&#x27;s the law anyway, why not make
it a benefit. Let them know that they are purchasing your
product or service RISK-FREE.
30) Instruct them to respond immediately.Â  Many people
just need to read those words, &quot;Act Now!&quot;Â  &quot;Order today!&quot;
&quot;Click Here to Instantly Place Your Order.&quot;Â  You&#x27;ve got them
this far, now tell them what you want them to do.Â  Get them
to &quot;Act Fast!&quot;Â  Have you ever heard a mail order commercial
on television that didn&#x27;t prompt the viewer to order right
way?
31) Include a post script.Â  People will always read the
P.S.Â  Always. In fact, the P.S. is one of the MOST IMPORTANT
parts of your sales letter. Why?Â  Because in many cases the
visitor at your website will scroll immediately down to the
end of your page to see how much it is going to cost.Â  A
P.S. is a perfect place to recap your offer, so when they
see your price tag, they will also see a very detailed
description of what they will receive for their money. Use
your P.S. to restate your offer in detail.
32) Include a second post script.Â  You better believe
if they read the first P.S., they will read a P.P.S.Â  Use
this post script to remind them of the deadline or offer
another bonus or point out some compelling factor that would
make them want to order.Â  I guarantee you they will read it.
Use these 32 tips and I guarantee you that you will see
a significant increase in the amount of responses you
receive from your sales letters.Â  In fact, it would be
impossible for your responses to not improve.

--- This Issue Contains Sites Who Will Trade Links With You! --- 
 
 
 32 Easy Ways To Breath New Life Into Any WebpageÂ Â Â Â Â Â Â  It&#x27;s true.
Ask the CEOs of Yahoo.com and America Online.Â  They&#x27;ll
tell you it&#x27;s true.Â  Send an email to Terry Dean or Allen
Says or Jim Daniels and ask them about it.Â  They&#x27;ll agree
100% that it&#x27;s true.Â  Don&#x27;t just take my word for it.
In fact, you can contact any of the 10,000 folks online
selling web marketing resources, and they will all tell you
emphatically, without question, no doubts whatsoever, that
it is absolutely true.
It&#x27;s true. ANYONE can earn a living online.Â  Really, they
can.Â  But, it takes several very important components to
join the 5% who are successful on the web.
Â Â Â Â  One of those necessities is a website.
Â Â Â Â  Now, your website does one of two things...
Â Â Â Â  ...it either makes the sale, or it doesn&#x27;t.
For 95% of online businesses, their websites simply do
not produce results.
And there is a very simple reason for poor performance.
Poor sales letters.
Does your website convince people to make a purchase?
If not, here are 32 easy ways to breathe new life into
your sales letter...
1) Write your sales letter with an individual in mind.
Go ahead and pick out someone, a real person to write your
sales letter to.Â  Doesn&#x27;t matter if it is grandma or your
next door neighbor or your cat.Â  Write your sales letter
just like you are writing it to them personally.Â  Why?
Because when your potential customer reads, it then it will
seem personal, almost like you wrote it with them in mind.
Too often, sales letters are written as if they were going
to be read to an audience rather than one person.Â  Keep your
sales letters personal, because one person at a time is
going to read them.
2) Use an illustration to get your point across.Â  In my
sales letters I have told stories about my car stalling on
the side of the road to illustrate the idea that we must
constantly add the fuel of advertising to keep our
businesses running. I have compared the hype of easily
making millions online to the chances of me riding bareback
across Montana on a grizzly bear.Â  Leads have read of how
getting to the top of an oak tree relates to aggressively
marketing online. People love a good story that pounds home
a solid message.Â  Tell stories that illustrate a point you
are trying to make.Â  Emphasize a benefit by sharing an
account from the &quot;real world.&quot;Â  It effectively creates
interest and further establishes the point.
3) Create an interest in the reader from the very first
line.Â  Your first line of the sales letter should
immediately create a desire in the reader to want to know
more.Â  Go back to the beginning of this article.Â  The first
words were, &quot;It&#x27;s true.&quot;Â  I can guarantee you that either
consciously or subconsciously you thought &quot;What&#x27;s true?&quot;
Immediately, your mind wanted to know what I was talking
about.Â  Before you even knew it you were right here, 8
paragraphs into this article.Â  Carefully craft your first
line.Â  If you can immediately get them wanting to know more,
you&#x27;ve got a winner.
4) Use bullets. People spend a lot of time reading bulleted
lists. In fact, they often reread them over and over. Use
bulleted lists to stress the benefits of your product or
service, to spell out exactly what is included in your offer.
Use an extra space in between each bullet to really highlight
each line and create a sense of more length to the list.
5) Launch into a bullet list immediately.Â  Shortly after
your opening line, immediately give the reader a bullet
list of benefits to absorb.Â  Hit them with your best shot.
Pull out the big guns and stress &quot;just a few of&quot; the most
important things the reader will discover.Â  By offering a
killer list early in your sales letter, you will automatically
create a desire in the reader to continue through your ad
copy.Â  After all, if they are already interested after the
first list of benefits, they will certainly be open to
finding out even more reasons why your product or service
will aid them.
6) Just let it all flow out.Â  Write down everything that
enters your mind as you are writing your sales letter. You
can edit it later.Â  If you just sit and start writing
everything you know about your product or service and how it
will benefit your customer, you will be amazed at how much
information floods your mind.Â  Write it ALL down.Â  Then read
through it - you&#x27;ll be able to add a lot more detail to
many of the points.Â  Edit it after you have exhausted all
of your ideas.
7) Make your sales letter personal.Â  Make sure that the
words &quot;you&quot; and &quot;your&quot; are at least 4:1 over &quot;I&quot; and &quot;my.&quot;
Your ad copy must be written about YOUR CUSTOMER not
yourself.Â  I&#x27;m not sure how the old advertising adage goes,
but it&#x27;s something like this, &quot;I don&#x27;t care a thing about
your lawn mower, I just care about my lawn.&quot;Â  Leads aren&#x27;t
interested in you or your products, they are interested in
themselves and their wants and needs.Â  When you are finished
with your sales letter and have uploaded it to a test
webpage, run a check at http://www.keywordcount.com and see
what the ratio between &quot;you&quot; and &quot;your&quot; versus references to
&quot;I,&quot; &quot;me,&quot; &quot;my,&quot; etc.Â  It&#x27;s a free service.Â  Make sure it&#x27;s
at least 4:1 in favor of the customer.
8) Write like you speak.Â  Forget all of those rules that
your grammar teacher taught you.Â  Write your sales letters
in everyday language, just like you would talk in person.
Don&#x27;t be afraid to begin sentences with &quot;And&quot; or &quot;Because.&quot;
Don&#x27;t worry about ending a sentence with a preposition.
Write like you speak. Your sales letter isn&#x27;t the great
American novel, so don&#x27;t write it like you are Ernest
Hemingway.
9) Use short paragraphs consisting of 2-4 sentences each.
Long copy works... but long paragraphs do not.Â  Use short
paragraphs that lead into the next paragraph. Don&#x27;t be
afraid to use short sentences.Â  Like this one.
Or this.
See what I mean?Â  Shorter paragraphs keep the interest of
the reader. Longer paragraphs cause eye strain and often
force the reader to get distracted.
10)Â  Stress the benefits, not the features.Â  Again,
readers want the burning question answered, &quot;What&#x27;s in it
forÂ  me?&quot;Â  What need is it going to meet? What want is it
going to fill?Â  How is your product or service going to be
of value or benefit to the reader?Â  Spell it out.Â  Don&#x27;t
focus on the features of your product or service , but
rather how those features will add value to the life of your
reader.
For example:Â  If you are selling automobile tires, you may
very well have the largest assortment of tires in the world,
but who cares? I don&#x27;t care about your selection. But, I
do care about keeping my 3-month-old baby girl safe while
we are traveling.Â  So, instead of focusing on your selection,
you focus on the fact that my baby girl can be kept safe
because you have a tire that will fit my car. You&#x27;re not
selling tires, you&#x27;re selling safety for my family. Stress
the benefits, not the features.
11) Keep the reader interested.Â  Some sales letters read
like they are a manual trying to explain to me how I can
perform some complicated surgery on my wife. They are
filled with words and phrases that I need a dictionary to
understand.Â  Unless you are writing to a very targeted
audience, avoid using technical language that many readers
might not understand. Keep it simple, using words, language
and information that are easy to understand and follow.
12) Target your sales letter.Â  When you are finished with
your final draft of the sales letter, target it to a
specific audience.Â  For example: If you are selling a &quot;work
at home&quot; product, then rewrite the sales letter by adding
words in the headlines and ad copy that are targeted towards
women who are homemakers.Â  Then, rewrite the same sales
letter and target it to college students.Â  Write another
letter targeting senior citizens.Â  Still another could be
written to high school teachers wanting to earn extra income
during summer vacation.Â  The possibilities are endless.
All you need to do is add a few words here and there in
your ad copy to make it appear that your product or service
is specifically designed for a target audience. &quot;Work only
5 hours a week,&quot; would become &quot;College Students, work only
5 hours a week.&quot;Â  Your sales letter is now targeted. Upload
all of the sales letters to separate pages on your website
(you could easily target 100&#x27;s of groups).
Then, simply advertise the targeted pages in targeted
mediums.Â  You could advertise the &quot;College Students&quot; page
in a campus ezine.Â  The &quot;Senior Citizens&quot; page could be
advertised at a retirement community message board.
By creating these targeted sales letters, you can literally
open up dozens of new groups to sell your existing product
to.Â  And, in their eyes, it looks like the product was a
match made for them.
13) Make your ad copy easy to follow.Â  Use short sentences
and paragraphs. Break up the sales letter with attention
grabbing headlines that lead into the next paragraph. One
thing that I have always found to work very well in sales
letters...
Â Â Â Â  ...is to use a pause like this.
Start the sentence on one line, leaving the reader wanting
to know more, and then finishing up on the next line. Also,
if you are going to use a sales letter that continues on
several different pages of your website, use a catchy hook
line at the end of each page to keep them clicking. &quot;Let&#x27;s
get you started down the road to success, shall we? CLICK
HERE to continue.&quot;
14) Use similes and metaphors for effect.Â  When the
customer purchases your product, they will generate &quot;a flood
of traffic that would make Noah start building another ark.&quot;
If they do not order today, then they will &quot;feel like a cat
that let the mouse get away.&quot;Â  Use words to create a picture
in the readers&#x27; mind.Â  When you think of Superman, what
comes to mind?Â  Immediately, we remember that he is &quot;faster
than a speeding bullet.&quot;Â  &quot;More powerful than a locomotive.&quot;
&quot;Able to leap tall buildings in a single bound.&quot; See how
word pictures stick in our minds?
15) Focus on one product or service.Â  Don&#x27;t try to sell
your customer multiple products at the same time. It only
confuses the reader.Â  Keep your ad copy directed at one
specific product or service.Â  Then, use other products and
services as back-end products.
16) Make it stand out.Â  Don&#x27;t kid yourself.Â  There are
hundreds, maybe thousands out there on the web doing the
same thing you are doing.Â  How will you stand out among the
crowd?Â  Your sales letter must inject personality. It must
breathe of originality.Â  Your product or service is
different. It&#x27;s not like all of the rest.Â  It is unique.
Right? Your sales letter must separate you from the
competition.Â  It must create a feeling of &quot;You won&#x27;t find
this anywhere else.&quot;
17) Be believable. &quot;Earn $54,000 in the next 24 hours!!!&quot;
Delete.Â  Good grief, do they think I am an idiot or
something?Â  Get real.Â  Don&#x27;t make outrageous claims that
are obviously not the truth.Â  You&#x27;ll ruin your reputation.
Let me tell you a simple universal fact that cannot be
reversed.Â  Once you have been branded a liar, you will
NEVER be anything but a liar.Â  It doesn&#x27;t matter if you
launch the most respectable, honest business available
anywhere, people will always have doubt because they
remember the crazy stuff you&#x27;ve said before.Â  Be believable.
Don&#x27;t exaggerate, mislead, stretch or distort the truth.
18) Be specific. Don&#x27;t generalize your information, but
rather be EXACT.Â  Instead of &quot;over 100 tips for losing
weight&quot; use &quot;124 tips for losing weight.&quot; By generalizing
information, it creates doubt and questions in the reader&#x27;s
mind.Â  &quot;What am I really getting here?Â  Does he even know?&quot;
When you use specific information, the reader begins to
think, &quot;This person must have counted.Â  I know exactly what
I can expect.&quot;Â  &quot;Platitudes and generalities roll off the
human understanding like water from a duck,&quot; wrote Claude
Hopkins in his classic book &quot;Scientific Advertising.&quot; &quot;They
leave no impression whatsoever.&quot;
19) Be complete. Tell the reader everything they would
want to know about your product or service.Â  Answer all of
their questions, anything they would want to consider before
making a purchase.Â  Think about it from their point of view.
Ask yourself, &quot;Why wouldn&#x27;t I buy this?&quot;Â  Then, address that
in your sales letter.Â  Remove anything that would keep the
reader from making the purchase.
20) Use testimonials to boost your sales.Â  Share actual
excerpts from what your current customers are saying about
your product or service. Many websites have an entire
section or even a separate page that has endorsements and
compliments listed.Â  Satisfied customers remove some of the
doubt in the mind of the reader.Â  &quot;If these people have
found a lot of value and benefit in the product, then I
probably will too.&quot;Â  Especially effective are testimonials
from respected, well known &quot;authorities&quot; within your target
field.
21) Use headlines over and over throughout the sales letter.
A headline isn&#x27;t just relegated to the beginning of your
ad copy.Â  Use them frequently -but don&#x27;t overuse.Â  A well-
placed headline re-grabs the reader&#x27;s attention, brings
them deeper into the letter, and readies them for the next
paragraph. You will want to spend as much time working on
your headlines as you do the entire sales letter.Â  They are
that important.
22) Avoid asking stupid questions.Â  &quot;Wouldn&#x27;t you like to
make $1,000,000 a year?&quot; &quot;Doesn&#x27;t that sound great?&quot; &quot;Would
you like to be as successful as I am?&quot;Â  Avoid any question
that insults the intelligence of your reader or makes them
feel like they are inferior.
23) Offer a freebie even if the customer doesn&#x27;t buy.
If the customer decides he or she isn&#x27;t going to make a
purchase, then you want to follow-up with them later to try
to influence them to buy in the future.Â  By offering a free
item, you canÂ  request their email address in order to
obtain the freebie.Â  By doing this, you can now follow-up
with the customer for a potential future sale.
Additionally, you can continue the sales process by having
your ad copy, banners, flyers, etc. within the free item.
And, of course, if your free item is a high quality, useful
product or service which impresses the customer, they
probably will be back as a customer soon.
24) Use bonuses to overwhelm the reader.Â  One of the things
that I have found very effective in writing sales letters
is to include bonus items that OUT-VALUE the actual product
I am offering.Â  Ginsu made this one famous. They were
selling a set of steak knives, but before the commercial
was finished, you had so many bonus items on the table
it was hard to refuse. Make sure you provide quality
bonuses and not some worthless, outdated junk that damages
the credibility of your main offer.
25) Use connective phrases like &quot;But wait, there&#x27;s more&quot;
and &quot;But that&#x27;s not all.&quot;Â  These phrases effectively lead
the reader from one paragraph to the next, particularly
when the next paragraph is a bullet list of benefits, or
leads into bonus items.Â  Again, the idea is MORE and MORE
value and benefits to the reader.
26) Always include a deadline.Â  By including a deadline,
you create a sense of urgency in the mind of the customer.
&quot;If I don&#x27;t order within 24 hours, then I won&#x27;t get the
bonuses.&quot;Â  &quot;Oh no, there are only 10 items remaining,
I&#x27;ve got to hurry.&quot;Â  Let the customer know what they will
be missing out on if they don&#x27;t make the deadline. Remember,
they won&#x27;t miss out on your products or bonuses, they will
miss out on all of the benefits of your products. Deadlines
are very effective.Â  Every sales letter should have one.
27) Tell them exactly how to order.Â  Be clear as to the
order process. Point them towards the order link.Â  Tell
them what methods you offer. (I.E. credit cards, checks,
etc.)Â  Make this process as simple and clear as can be.
If it takes more than 2 steps, most people won&#x27;t continue.
28)Â  Explain when the product will be delivered.Â  How
quickly will the order be processed?Â  When will the order be
available?Â  Let the customer know exactly what they can
expect when they place their order.Â  The more specific you
can be here, the better.Â  Let them know that you have a
system in place.Â  &quot;Operators are standing by.&quot;Â  Their order
will be handled properly.Â  Tell them.
29) Offer a money back guarantee.Â  Take away their last
reason to hold back.Â  Offer a &quot;no questions asked&quot; 30 day
guarantee.Â  Most people may not realize this, but in most
cases, it&#x27;s the law of the land.Â  You are REQUIRED to give
them their money back if they are not satisfied with the
product or service.Â  Since it&#x27;s the law anyway, why not make
it a benefit. Let them know that they are purchasing your
product or service RISK-FREE.
30) Instruct them to respond immediately.Â  Many people
just need to read those words, &quot;Act Now!&quot;Â  &quot;Order today!&quot;
&quot;Click Here to Instantly Place Your Order.&quot;Â  You&#x27;ve got them
this far, now tell them what you want them to do.Â  Get them
to &quot;Act Fast!&quot;Â  Have you ever heard a mail order commercial
on television that didn&#x27;t prompt the viewer to order right
way?
31) Include a post script.Â  People will always read the
P.S.Â  Always. In fact, the P.S. is one of the MOST IMPORTANT
parts of your sales letter. Why?Â  Because in many cases the
visitor at your website will scroll immediately down to the
end of your page to see how much it is going to cost.Â  A
P.S. is a perfect place to recap your offer, so when they
see your price tag, they will also see a very detailed
description of what they will receive for their money. Use
your P.S. to restate your offer in detail.
32) Include a second post script.Â  You better believe
if they read the first P.S., they will read a P.P.S.Â  Use
this post script to remind them of the deadline or offer
another bonus or point out some compelling factor that would
make them want to order.Â  I guarantee you they will read it.
Use these 32 tips and I guarantee you that you will see
a significant increase in the amount of responses you
receive from your sales letters.Â  In fact, it would be
impossible for your responses to not improve.

5 Ways to Create a Unique Website
=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=-=Internet users have no doubt seen several websites of identical
design. This is often because webmasters do not have the
ability or time to design and create unique websites. These
five points will help you come up with a one-of-a-kind design
for your website.1. Navigation SystemDeciding on a navigation system should be one of the first
things to consider when designing your website. Visitors
usually look for a way to get to the information they want
before reading anything else. I would suggest putting your
navigation system in two areas. Look at WebmasterBase 
(http://www.webmasterbase.com) for example. There is a list 
of categories on the left, and information about the network 
on the top bar.It is not recommended that you place your navigation at the
bottom of the page because:- People see the top of the page first
- If you have a lot of content on one page, the navigation gets
   pushed to bottom of the page... away from the visitor.2. Color SchemeWhen you are choosing colors, make sure they contrast. For
example, do not use bright green and florescent pink. Some
colors that are often used are white on black or black on
white.A wonderful utility that is web-based and free is Color Schemer
(http://www.godigitalstudios.com/www/color/), created by Aaron
Epstein. Many other programs exist that can assist you in
choosing colors. Try doing a search on FreewareFiles 
(http://www.freewarefiles.com), Tucows (http://www.tucows.com),
or Download.com (http://www.download.com) for &quot;color schemer.&quot;3. Content WindowThis is the brain of the website. Make sure that the text here
is easy to read and understand.Also, provide your own original content. People do not want to
hear what somebody else has to say. They want to hear what 
you want to say! While it might be time consuming to write
your own content, it sure is worth it.To find content for your website, try searching at
EzineArticles (http://www.ezinearticles.com). Other people may
have used the articles, but if you become good friends with the
authors, they may send you new content as they write it. Be
careful though. Do not use too much content from other 
authors, as mentioned above.4. Font(s)Several types of fonts exist. The first one I will discuss is
fixed width or monospace, similar to Courier or Courier New.
Fixed width fonts are good for displaying code (like HTML.)Another type of font is called sans serif. It is similar to
Arial (Windows) or Helvetica (Macintosh). This font can be 
used for long pieces of text.The final type of font is called serif because it has serifs,
or curves, in each letter. Often, this font is called Times
(Windows) or Georgia (Macintosh). This font, like sans serif,
is often used for long blocks of text. It is preferred because
its rounded edges lead the reader to the next letter, making it
easier to read.5. AdvertisingAh, everybody&#x27;s favorite... advertising! This is one of thing 
that almost every website has. The most common type is what 
is known as &quot;banner&quot; advertising. It is called that because it is 
long and thin (like a paper banner.) How long you ask? The 
generic kind is 468 pixels wide by 60 pixels high.New sizes however are being used. Take for example the new
style called &quot;skyscraper&quot; -- so called for a reason: it looks
like a skyscraper. It is tall (about 425 pixels) but not too
wide (about 100-125 pixels.)Finding a way to place advertising can be tricky. Banners 
used to be placed (and still are) only at the top of the page.
Nevertheless, banners are starting to be placed in the middle
of the page, at the bottom, or along the side.    ConclusionThese are only five things to consider when designing a
website. There are many others however. Let me know what 
you find that helps you create a unique website. My email 
address is mailto:corbb@justforwebmasters.com.-------------------------------------
About the Author...Written by Corbb O&#x27;Connor. Corbb is the Webmaster of
http://www.justforwebmasters.com, a website that focuses on
teaching Webmasters how to create, gain traffic to, and make
money from their own websites. He is also the editor of a FREE
bi-weekly newsletter entitled, &quot;The Guided Webmaster.&quot; To
subscribe now, send an email to:
mailto:subscribe@justforwebmasters.com?subject=article-subscribe. 
Information about this newsletter can be obtained from his website.

 5 Ways to Create a Unique Websiteï¿½ï¿½Internet users have no doubt seen several websites of
identical design. This is often because webmasters do not
have the ability or time to design and create unique websites.
These five points will help you come up with a one-of-a-kind
design for your website.
1. Navigation System
Deciding on a navigation system should be one of the first
things to consider when designing your website. Visitors
usually look for a way to get to the information they want
before reading anything else. I would suggest putting your
navigation system in two areas. Look at WebmasterBase
(http://www.webmasterbase.com)
for example. There is a list
of categories on the left, and information about the network
on the top bar.
It is not recommended that you place your navigation at the
bottom of the page because:
- People see the top of the page first
- If you have a lot of content on one page, the navigation
Â Â Â  gets pushed to bottom of the page... away from the
visitor.
2. Color Scheme
When you are choosing colors, make sure they contrast.
For example, do not use bright green and florescent pink.
Some colors that are often used are white on black or black
on white.
A wonderful utility that is web-based and free is Color
Schemer (http://www.godigitalstudios.com/www/color/),
created by Aaron Epstein. Many other programs exist that
can assist you in choosing colors. Try doing a search on
FreewareFiles (http://www.freewarefiles.com),
Tucows
(http://www.tucows.com), or Download.com
(http://www.download.com) for
&quot;color schemer.&quot;
3. Content Window
This is the brain of the website. Make sure that the text
here is easy to read and understand.
Also, provide your own original content. People do not want
to hear what somebody else has to say. They want to hear
what you want to say! While it might be time consuming to
write your own content, it sure is worth it.
To find content for your website, try searching at
EzineArticles (http://www.ezinearticles.com).
Other people
may have used the articles, but if you become good friends
with the authors, they may send you new content as they
write it. Be careful though. Do not use too much content
from other authors, as mentioned above.
4. Font(s)
Several types of fonts exist. The first one I will discuss is
fixed width or monospace, similar to Courier or Courier New.
Fixed width fonts are good for displaying code (like HTML.)
Another type of font is called sans serif. It is similar to
Arial (Windows) or Helvetica (Macintosh). This font can
be used for long pieces of text.
The final type of font is called serif because it has serifs,
or curves, in each letter. Often, this font is called Times
(Windows) or Georgia (Macintosh). This font, like sans serif,
is often used for long blocks of text. It is preferred because
its rounded edges lead the reader to the next letter, making
it easier to read.
5. Advertising
Ah, everybody&#x27;s favorite... advertising! This is one of thing
that almost every website has. The most common type is
what is known as &quot;banner&quot; advertising. It is called that
because it is long and thin (like a paper banner.) How long
you ask? The generic kind is 468 pixels wide by 60 pixels high.
New sizes however are being used. Take for example the
new style called &quot;skyscraper&quot; -- so called for a reason: it
looks like a skyscraper. It is tall (about 425 pixels) but not
too wide (about 100-125 pixels.)
Finding a way to place advertising can be tricky. Banners
used to be placed (and still are) only at the top of the
page.Â  Nevertheless, banners are starting to be placed in
the middle of the page, at the bottom, or along the side.
Conclusion
These are only five things to consider when designing a
website. There are many others however. Let me know
what you find that helps you create a unique website. My
email address is mailto:corbb@justforwebmasters.com.

urgent business assistance mr james kofi project implementation ministry of energy and mineral resources p . o . box 745 , accra . ghana email : kofi 51 @ zwallet . com dear sir , it is my pleasure to write this letter to you . first , i must ask your utmost confidentiality in this transaction by virtue of it being utterly confidential . i am mr . robert kokoh , a member of the ghana export promotion council ( gepc ) . i am currently in europe on a one year traning course . i was also with the government delegation to your country during a bilateral trade conference . i have decided to seek your assistance and co - operation in the execution of a business transaction described here under for the benefit of all parties and hope that you keep it confidential because of the nature of the transaction . we basically seek your assistance to front in the transfer of us $ 30 , 500 , 000 . 00 ( thirty million , five hundred thousand united states dollars only ) . source : i was working as a director of project implementation within the ministry of energy and mineral resources and with co - operation of three other top officials , we have in our possession an overdue payment in us funds . the said funds represents certain percentage of the contract value executed on behalf of my ministry by a foreign contracting firm , which we the officials over - invoiced by a total amount of us $ 30 , 500 , 000 . 00 ( thirty million five hundred thousand united states dollars ) . though the actual contract cost has been paid to the original contractor , leaving the excess balance unclaimed . but by virtue of us being civil servants we cannot acquire this fund in our names , therefore i was delegated as a matter of trust by my colleagues to look for an overseas partner into whose account we would transfer the funds hence we are writing you this letter . our government is determined to pay foreign contractors all debts owned so as to maintain continued good relations with both the foreign governments and non government financial agencies . we have decided to include our bills for approval with the co - operation of some officials at the department of finance and the central bank of ghana ( cbg ) . we hereby seek your assistance to act as the beneficiary of the unclaimed funds since we are not allowed to operate a foreign account . you will provide us with a good front and partnership to process the remittance of the money to you as a form of contract payment . this process will be an internal arrangement with the departments concerned and we will handle this here , your duty is to act as the contractor and have the contract sum paid to you . i have the authority of my partners to invite you into this matter and with your acceptance of our invitation , your share as compensation will be 20 % , and 70 % will be for us and 10 % will be for the settlement of taxation and other miscellaneous expenses incurred . the business itself is 100 % safe , fail proof and risk - free , provided you treat it with utmost confidentiality and you follow our instructions carefully and religiously . your area of business specialization does not matter as it cannot hinder the successful execution of this transaction . i hope i can put my confidence in you in this matter . please contact me by return email to indicate your willingness and capability to assist us . thank you for your anticipated co - operation and i await the receipt of our message to enable me bring you into the complete picture of the transaction . yours faithfully , mr james kofi .
